CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/benchmark/compiler.mojo  (build: inlined_assembly['', NoneType, 'r,~{memory}', True] is a subscript whose index is a tuple. A value here is one 64-bit word and a list is a flat blob of words, so a tuple index has no representation on this path. It is one of two things: a lookup keyed by the tuple, which is lowered only when the base is a known dict and compares the key element-wise; or a two-dimensional index, which needs a row stride the source never states. Refused rather than computing a plausible flat index, and rather than using the tuple blob's own address as the index — that builds, runs, and returns an element nobody asked for.)

A generic template's instantiations ARE compiled into that module's library when an importer asks for them (`formal/monomorph.py`), so this call is one that asked for none — it names no type argument, or names one that is a value rather than a type, or spells the template as `module.FormatStruct`; spell it as `FormatStruct[<a type>](…)` and the library will carry the instantiation. Write the operation in this module, or call a public function that does it — which is the same program with a definition this image can bind)
CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/builtin/float_literal.mojo  (build: FloatLiteral___int__: `self.__int_literal__().__int__(…)` cannot be lowered: dispatch here is BY NAME, so a method call is lifted to `FloatLiteral___int__(receiver, …)` from the name alone — and `self.__int_literal__()` is not a bare name, so there is no name to lift from. What is missing is the receiver's TYPE: this path has no inference that answers "which struct does `self.__int_literal__()` hold?", and dispatch is by name rather than by type, so the callee cannot be named. Declaring it is the same program with an answer — give the receiver a local of a declared struct type, or bind it to one (`var x = T()`, `def f(v: T)`) so the lift has a name to work from. A caller-spelled subscript receiver is a narrower question than it looks: where the source states the element type — a list literal of `T()`, or a parameter declared `List[T]` — `receiver_struct` answers it and the lift runs (`formal/build.py::_subscript_receiver_target`). It is only the element type nothing in the source names that reaches this refusal.)

CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/builtin/len.mojo  (build: a `...` stands where this path needs instructions to emit, and there are none: a formal image is a compiled program, so every function it lowers must have a body. This is the language's own no-implementation marker — write the function, or declare it as a `trait` method, which is a declaration of an interface and so has no body to lower (a `...` in a trait method builds on both architectures, and a `...` in a plain function is refused whether or not anything ever calls it))
CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/builtin/none.mojo  (build: writer.write_string() is a method on a Writer — a multi-field struct, so on this path the receiver is the ADDRESS of a frame of 8-byte slots, not a file descriptor. It is not the `write` that is lowered: that one is honest only because open(...) makes its receiver a descriptor, and nothing here establishes that a Writer is one. Lowering it as a write would pass a frame address as fd(2): the syscall returns EBADF, nothing checks it, and the program's output is missing rather than wrong-looking. Refused rather than emitted as a call to a symbol spelled 'writer.write_string', which is what this used to do: the image built and then died in the loader)

CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/collections/type_dict.mojo  (build: Self._index reads a `comptime` class attribute of TypeDict, whose value is `Self.keys.try_index(key)` — and what it reads is 'keys', a PARAMETER of TypeDict. A parameter's value belongs to an INSTANTIATION: the use site supplies it as an argument (`TypeDict[…, <value>, …]`), and that argument is a value, not a literal written in this class body, so there is nothing here to write somewhere else. This path compiles one image for every instantiation — `doc/ABI.md` §Generics makes each instantiation a separate boundary symbol and there is no monomorphizer here — so at the class body there is no instantiation, and `Self.keys` names a parameter nothing has supplied. That is a different fact from a value this path cannot compute, and the two have different next steps: this one is closed by binding the parameter values at the instantiation site, not by editing the expression)

The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. Make the initializer a literal (or an expression of literals and other `comptime` names), or bind it with an ordinary `var` and read it at run time)

CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/origin/__init__.mojo  (build: the module-level comptime binding 'AnyOrigin' is initialized from an MLIR attribute template: __mlir_attr[`#lit.any.origin : !lit.origin<`, +mut._mlir_value, `>`] assembles an MLIR attribute from a template of backtick-quoted literal fragments and compile-time sub-expressions: it is not a subscript, and there is no MLIR on this path for the template to become. Refused rather than materialized as a container, which would turn an attribute into a pointer to a frame blob and disagree with the compiler that does have MLIR. Module-level `comptime` bindings are not part of the function-body expression walk on this path, so nothing used to refuse this and the binding read as an undefined name at every use site.)

The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. A generic template's instantiations ARE compiled into that module's library when an importer asks for them (`formal/monomorph.py`), so this call is one that asked for none — it names no type argument, or names one that is a value rather than a type, or spells the template as `module.dealloc`; spell it as `dealloc[<a type>](…)` and the library will carry the instantiation. Write the operation in this module, or call a public function that does it — which is the same program with a definition this image can bind)

CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/stat/__init__.mojo  (build: __init__.mojo imports '.stat', which cannot be built either: stat.mojo: formal dylib has no public functions: stat.mojo exports nothing under doc/ABI.md's rules: every public function in it is a GENERIC template (S_ISBLK, S_ISCHR, S_ISDIR, S_ISFIFO, S_ISLNK, S_ISREG …), and doc/ABI.md is explicit that a generic is not a single boundary symbol — each INSTANTIATION is, under its own mangled name. This path instantiates them on demand (`formal/monomorph.py`), so reaching this message means nothing asked this module for an instantiation of any of them: call one with its type arguments spelled (`S_ISBLK[Int](…)`) and the library you import will carry the instantiation this message says is missing. A BARE call still names no instantiation, and exporting the template under its base name instead would be wrong rather than conservative: one trie entry cannot be two instantiations, so a call with different type arguments would silently bind the first one's body.)

That module does not export it, and the reason is `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule rather than anything about this call: a name with a leading `_` is private, a generic template is not one symbol but one per instantiation (`_get_kgen_string[asm]()` is the measured case — both at once), an overload has no single symbol, and a C library name like `exit` or `write` is provided by libSystem. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/time/__init__.mojo  (build: __init__.mojo imports '.time', which cannot be built either: time.mojo: _gettime_as_nsec_unix: 'CompilationTarget' is imported from `std.sys`, and it is a module-level name of another module. This path compiles an import into a dylib, and a dylib publishes FUNCTIONS and folded CONSTANTS — a name whose value the build knows (`std.sys.K = 1`, `std.sys.S = "x"`, a `comptime` constant) lowers, and a call `std.sys.fn(...)` lowers. What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE: there is no storage for one here, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns — and a list, a stream object or a function used as a value is one of those, not a constant. So this name's value is a real global with nowhere to live, which is a property of the value model rather than of this call: `std.sys.fn(...)` where `std.sys` publishes that function as a function is the same program with a representation. bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md records the design and what would have to be true to close it)

CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/traits/copyable.mojo  (build: copyable.mojo imports 'std.builtin.rebind', which cannot be built either: `FormatStruct` is called, and it is imported from `std.format._utils`, so the call has to bind a symbol `std.format._utils` exports. That module does not export it, and the reason is `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule rather than anything about this call: a name with a leading `_` is private, a generic template is not one symbol but one per instantiation (`_get_kgen_string[asm]()` is the measured case — both at once), an overload has no single symbol, and a C library name like `exit` or `write` is provided by libSystem. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. A generic template's instantiations ARE compiled into that module's library when an importer asks for them (`formal/monomorph.py`), so this call is one that asked for none — it names no type argument, or names one that is a value rather than a type, or spells the template as `module.FormatStruct`; spell it as `FormatStruct[<a type>](…)` and the library will carry the instantiation. Write the operation in this module, or call a public function that does it — which is the same program with a definition this image can bind)

CODEGEN: ../new-modular/Mojo/stdlib/std/utils/_serialize.mojo  (build: p.unsafe_load() is Mojo's name for the UNCHECKED READ through a pointer — on a pointer the same operation `value()` is, at the same declared width, which is why it is in this table and reaches the same `dereference_lowering`. An ARGUMENT makes it a different construct: it reads at an offset from the receiver, and the address and the load are one decision there of which only the load half is answered, so `dereference_operands_refusal` names that rather than reporting an argument count. The receiver is not established to be a pointer here, and the answer a pointer would give is a load whose width the pointee decides: q = p.unsafe_value() makes q the value at p, which here is a Scalar[dtype] — a scalar, not a pointer, so q.value() is not a dereference. Refused rather than emitted at the only width this path could choose without one, which is 8 bytes: that over-reads a 1-byte pointee by seven bytes, returns a plausible number assembled from whatever follows it, and faults outright at a page edge. Declaring the pointee — `var p: Pointer[UInt8]`, a parameter annotated `Pointer[Int32]`, or a pointer-valued `external_call["sym", Pointer[T]]` — is what makes it answerable)

NOT-ANSWERABLE/HOST-IMPORT: ast_rewriter.py  (build: ast_rewriter.py imports 'fire_compiler', which cannot be built either: fire_compiler.py imports 'importlib', which is a host module (CPython standard library), which has no Mojo source for this backend to compile)
CODEGEN: build_mojo_cli.py  (build: create_mojo_cli: stat.S_IXUSR reads 'S_IXUSR' out of the imported module `stat`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `stat.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `stat.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `S_IXUSR` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `stat` publishes: ST_ATIME, ST_CTIME, ST_DEV, ST_GID, ST_INO, ST_MODE, ST_MTIME, ST_NLINK, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)

NOT-ANSWERABLE/HOST-IMPORT: consolidate_string_pool.py  (build: consolidate_string_pool.py imports 'collections', which is a host module (CPython standard library), which has no Mojo source for this backend to compile; the record the callers here want is a STRUCT: declare it with its fields, and read them as fields. Its only module-level name is `namedtuple`, which builds a TYPE at run time, and there is no word on this path that denotes a type (doc/ABI.md, Generics), so `namedtuple("Census", "ok detail …")` asks for something that cannot be lowered even in principle — which is why "write a struct instead" is a complete answer for a record whose fields the compiler can see and is NOT an answer for one it cannot. Measured: five files in this repository's own tooling import it (`formal/lean.py`'s `Census`, `formal/model.py`'s `InitShape`, `tools/formal_sweep.py`'s three). `Counter` is a dict rather than a record, so it is the other question: see bugs/FORMAL_listdir_no_run_time_sequence.md)

CODEGEN: determinism_trace.py  (build: os.environ.get(): `os.environ` is not a module with a library on this link line — `environ` is a FUNCTION of `os`, so `os.environ.get()` is a call through a VALUE and there is no symbol for it to bind. A dylib publishes functions, not the objects they are called on, so an operation on a value a module hands back cannot be spelled as a member of it; the operations on it are published as FUNCTIONS of `os`, and those beginning `environ_` are they: environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, environ_del, environ_find, environ_free, environ_get, environ_get_or, … — call one of those, passing whatever the `environ`-returning function hands back. bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md §(2) records why a value cannot cross a dylib boundary at all)

Its only module-level name is `namedtuple`, which builds a TYPE at run time, and there is no word on this path that denotes a type (doc/ABI.md, Generics), so `namedtuple("Census", "ok detail …")` asks for something that cannot be lowered even in principle — which is why "write a struct instead" is a complete answer for a record whose fields the compiler can see and is NOT an answer for one it cannot. CODEGEN: formal/x86_64_decode.py  (build: constructing Insn spreads `kw` with `**`, and a spread's KEYS are not knowable at the construction site: the mapping is a value this path cannot enumerate, so there is no saying which field each key names, and `S(a=1, **{'a': 2})` is a run-time `TypeError` in CPython too — the only way to tell it from `S(a=1, **{'b': 2})` is to know the keys. A spread of a dict LITERAL is un-spread and answered like any other keyword (both keys and values are in the source), so write the keywords out, or pass the mapping as an ordinary parameter this path can carry. Note that a `**`-PARAMETER cannot be the operand here either: a formal value is one 64-bit word and this target has no variadic ABI, so a callee cannot read its own `**kwargs` — which is the capability to build before a spread through one is answerable.)

CODEGEN: mlir.py  (build: line 284: `ValueError` is a handler arm with a body this path cannot put in the image, so it is refused rather than dropped: `formal` has no exception unwinder, so no edge runs from a raise site into an arm — a `raise` flushes the enclosing `finally` clauses and exits the process — and every statement in this arm (`ReturnStmt`) would be absent from the program that runs. Its effects would be silently missing: the image would build, exit 0, and not be the program you wrote, which is the one failure a value this compiler cannot detect on its own. An arm whose body is `pass`, `raise`, `continue` or `break` still builds — nothing is lost by leaving it out. Otherwise: move the work into the `try` body or after the statement (a `finally` if it is cleanup for the success path), or end the arm with `raise` if the program is meant to fail there.)
CODEGEN: module_loader.py  (build: _find_stdlib_path: os.environ reads 'environ' out of the imported module `os`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `os.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `os.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `environ` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `os` publishes: chdir, chmod, curdir, devnull, environ, environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)

CODEGEN: mojo/backend_gimple/device_glue.py  (build: iterating a string is a CONTAINER operation on a char *, and a char * has no count. This lowering reads eight bytes at offset 0 of the iterable and calls the result the element COUNT — that is a blob's header word, and a string's first eight bytes are TEXT: measured on both architectures, `for c in "abc"` returned 97 on x86-64 ('a' as the count) and died of SIGBUS on arm64. `len(s)` and `s[i]` both work and are unaffected; to walk the characters, build a list of them first ([c for c in <a list>]) or iterate that list)

CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: mojo/backend_gimple/emit_resolve.py  (build: emit_resolve.py imports 'determinism_trace', which cannot be built either: determinism_trace.py: os.environ.get(): `os.environ` is not a module with a library on this link line — `environ` is a FUNCTION of `os`, so `os.environ.get()` is a call through a VALUE and there is no symbol for it to bind. A dylib publishes functions, not the objects they are called on, so an operation on a value a module hands back cannot be spelled as a member of it; the operations on it are published as FUNCTIONS of `os`, and those beginning `environ_` are they: environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, environ_del, environ_find, environ_free, environ_get, environ_get_or, … — call one of those, passing whatever the `environ`-returning function hands back. bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md §(2) records why a value cannot cross a dylib boundary at all)

A dylib publishes functions, not the objects they are called on, so an operation on a value a module hands back cannot be spelled as a member of it; the operations on it are published as FUNCTIONS of `os`, and those beginning `environ_` are they: environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, environ_del, environ_find, environ_free, environ_get, environ_get_or, … — call one of those, passing whatever the `environ`-returning function hands back. bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md §(2) records why a value cannot cross a dylib boundary at all)

CODEGEN: regex_compile.py  (build: atom is assigned _Parser_parse_atom(self) in _Parser_parse_repeat(), and atom also holds the address of a Repeat frame — Repeat() binds it to one, and this path has no way to say that a later binding changes what the name is. Every field access through atom is already lowered as a load at `[base + 8·slot]` on the strength of the first binding, so after this store the load reads address `_Parser_parse_atom(self) + 8·slot`: measured, the program builds, runs, and dies with SIGSEGV (exit 139) on both architectures, which is a fault rather than a wrong number only because the word happened not to be mapped. A value that may be a frame is one of three things and this is not any of them: a CONSTRUCTION of a framed struct (`atom = S(...)`), a COPY of another holder (`atom = other`), or a parameter of a method of a framed struct. Use a different name for the word, or copy the value out of atom first)

CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: run_type_system_tests.py  (build: run_type_system_tests.py imports 'type_system', which cannot be built either: type_system.py: a Type frame address is passed to isinstance(), which is lowered as an operation on a VALUE: it wants the object itself, and on this path a multi-field struct has no value form — its receiver is a pointer to a frame of 8-byte slots, so what would arrive is the address isinstance() would then dereference as one. Not a missing layout: a wrong category of argument. Give it a field (`isinstance(self.n)`) or copy the value out first)
CODEGEN: runtime/stdlib_wrapper.mojo  (build: main: `now` is called, and it is imported from `time`, so the call has to bind a symbol `time` exports. That module does not export it, and the reason is `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule rather than anything about this call: a name with a leading `_` is private, a generic template is not one symbol but one per instantiation (`_get_kgen_string[asm]()` is the measured case — both at once), an overload has no single symbol, and a C library name like `exit` or `write` is provided by libSystem. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. A generic template's instantiations ARE compiled into that module's library when an importer asks for them (`formal/monomorph.py`), so this call is one that asked for none — it names no type argument, or names one that is a value rather than a type, or spells the template as `module.now`; spell it as `now[<a type>](…)` and the library will carry the instantiation. Write the operation in this module, or call a public function that does it — which is the same program with a definition this image can bind)

CODEGEN: t1.mojo  (build: sys.exit(): `sys` is a linked module but it exports no `exit`, so the call has no symbol to bind. What it does export: api_version, byteorder, flush_output, get_coroutine_origin_tracking_depth, get_int_max_str_digits, getdefaultencoding, getfilesystemencodeerrors, getfilesystemencoding, …. A name `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule excludes (a leading `_`, a generic template, an overload, or a C library symbol such as `exit` or `write`) is not advertised, so a module that defines one cannot be called by that name. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md 1.1; a module that needs to publish mutable state rather than functions is a different gap, written down in bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md)
CODEGEN: t_argv.mojo  (build: main: sys.argv reads 'argv' out of the imported module `sys`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `sys.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `sys.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `argv` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `sys` publishes: api_version, byteorder, flush_output, get_coroutine_origin_tracking_depth, get_int_max_str_digits, getdefaultencoding, getfilesystemencodeerrors, getfilesystemencoding, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)

CODEGEN: test_arm64_emission.py  (build: line 432: `TestFailure` as e is a handler arm with a body this path cannot put in the image, so it is refused rather than dropped: `formal` has no exception unwinder, so no edge runs from a raise site into an arm — a `raise` flushes the enclosing `finally` clauses and exits the process — and every statement in this arm (`AugAssignStmt`) would be absent from the program that runs. Its effects would be silently missing: the image would build, exit 0, and not be the program you wrote, which is the one failure a value this compiler cannot detect on its own. An arm whose body is `pass`, `raise`, `continue` or `break` still builds — nothing is lost by leaving it out. Otherwise: move the work into the `try` body or after the statement (a `finally` if it is cleanup for the success path), or end the arm with `raise` if the program is meant to fail there.)

CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: test_gimple_runner.py  (build: test_gimple_runner.py imports 'build_config', which cannot be built either: module_loader.py: __module_body__: os.path reads 'path' out of the imported module `os`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `os.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `os.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `path` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `os` publishes: chdir, chmod, curdir, devnull, environ, environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)
CODEGEN: test_interp_oracle.py  (build: line 242: `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` is a handler arm with a body this path cannot put in the image, so it is refused rather than dropped: `formal` has no exception unwinder, so no edge runs from a raise site into an arm — a `raise` flushes the enclosing `finally` clauses and exits the process — and every statement in this arm (`ReturnStmt`) would be absent from the program that runs. Its effects would be silently missing: the image would build, exit 0, and not be the program you wrote, which is the one failure a value this compiler cannot detect on its own. An arm whose body is `pass`, `raise`, `continue` or `break` still builds — nothing is lost by leaving it out. Otherwise: move the work into the `try` body or after the statement (a `finally` if it is cleanup for the success path), or end the arm with `raise` if the program is meant to fail there.)
CODEGEN: test_llm/dumb_gemm.mojo  (build: [FloatLiteral] * m * k is a REPETITION, and this path can only lower one whose count it can read while emitting: the result is len(xs) x n element slots in the function's own frame, and there is no heap to grow into, so the reservation is made before anything runs and m * k is not a number this build can see. Write the count as a literal, or build the list at run time (append into a list literal sized for what the program needs), or split it across two functions so each gets its own budget)

NOT-ANSWERABLE/TARGET-LIMIT: test_sqlite3.mojo  (build: mojo_sqlite3_open is an entry point of the gimple backend's C runtime (the `mojo_*` ABI declared in runtime/*.h), and a formal image is freestanding: it links libSystem, whatever modules it imports, and nothing of runtime/ unless it names an entry point here — in which case `formal/build.py` puts the per-architecture `mojo_*` library on the link line for it. Its values are one 64-bit word. It could not be answered by linking that library either: its return value is `void *`, and that is an address rather than a value. An ARGUMENT can be one: this path carries the word and hands it straight back to the library that made it, never looking at what is behind it. A return is the other way round — the caller is the one who would have to read it, and the declaration does not say what is there. `void *` is spelled the same for a handle a program only passes on and for a container it reads elements out of, and a `MojoStr *` is a struct in the C heap, which a freestanding image has no allocator for. Either way the answer is a box, and it is not a box this path can produce. Refused rather than emitted as a call to a symbol nothing defines, which is what this used to do — the image built and then died in the loader with "Symbol not found".)

NOT-ANSWERABLE/HOST-IMPORT: test_x86_64_containers.py  (build: test_x86_64_containers.py imports 'formal.build', which cannot be built either: build.py imports 'copy', which is a host module (CPython standard library), which has no Mojo source for this backend to compile; a field-wise copy of a type the COMPILER KNOWS at the call site is a struct construction: build a new one and assign the fields. NOT-ANSWERABLE/HOST-IMPORT: test_x86_64_decode.py  (build: test_x86_64_decode.py imports 'formal.x86_64', which cannot be built either: model.py imports 'collections', which is a host module (CPython standard library), which has no Mojo source for this backend to compile; the record the callers here want is a STRUCT: declare it with its fields, and read them as fields. Its only module-level name is `namedtuple`, which builds a TYPE at run time, and there is no word on this path that denotes a type (doc/ABI.md, Generics), so `namedtuple("Census", "ok detail …")` asks for something that cannot be lowered even in principle — which is why "write a struct instead" is a complete answer for a record whose fields the compiler can see and is NOT an answer for one it cannot. Measured: five files in this repository's own tooling import it (`formal/lean.py`'s `Census`, `formal/model.py`'s `InitShape`, `tools/formal_sweep.py`'s three). `Counter` is a dict rather than a record, so it is the other question: see bugs/FORMAL_listdir_no_run_time_sequence.md)

NOT-ANSWERABLE/HOST-IMPORT: test_x86_64_encoders.py  (build: test_x86_64_encoders.py imports 'formal.x86_64', which cannot be built either: model.py imports 'collections', which is a host module (CPython standard library), which has no Mojo source for this backend to compile; the record the callers here want is a STRUCT: declare it with its fields, and read them as fields. Its only module-level name is `namedtuple`, which builds a TYPE at run time, and there is no word on this path that denotes a type (doc/ABI.md, Generics), so `namedtuple("Census", "ok detail …")` asks for something that cannot be lowered even in principle — which is why "write a struct instead" is a complete answer for a record whose fields the compiler can see and is NOT an answer for one it cannot. Measured: five files in this repository's own tooling import it (`formal/lean.py`'s `Census`, `formal/model.py`'s `InitShape`, `tools/formal_sweep.py`'s three). `Counter` is a dict rather than a record, so it is the other question: see bugs/FORMAL_listdir_no_run_time_sequence.md)
CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: tools/ab_compare.py  (build: ab_compare.py imports 'ab_filelist', which cannot be built either: module_loader.py: __module_body__: os.path reads 'path' out of the imported module `os`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `os.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `os.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `path` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `os` publishes: chdir, chmod, curdir, devnull, environ, environ_clear, environ_copy, environ_count, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)

CODEGEN: tools/audit_determinism.py  (build: __module_body__: sys.argv reads 'argv' out of the imported module `sys`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `sys.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `sys.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `argv` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `sys` publishes: api_version, byteorder, flush_output, get_coroutine_origin_tracking_depth, get_int_max_str_digits, getdefaultencoding, getfilesystemencodeerrors, getfilesystemencoding, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)
CODEGEN/DEPENDENCY: tools/ab_run_one.py  (build: ab_run_one.py imports 'memslot', which cannot be built either: memslot.py: line 507: `ValueError` as e is a handler arm with a body this path cannot put in the image, so it is refused rather than dropped: `formal` has no exception unwinder, so no edge runs from a raise site into an arm — a `raise` flushes the enclosing `finally` clauses and exits the process — and every statement in this arm (`ExprStmt`) would be absent from the program that runs. Its effects would be silently missing: the image would build, exit 0, and not be the program you wrote, which is the one failure a value this compiler cannot detect on its own. An arm whose body is `pass`, `raise`, `continue` or `break` still builds — nothing is lost by leaving it out. Otherwise: move the work into the `try` body or after the statement (a `finally` if it is cleanup for the success path), or end the arm with `raise` if the program is meant to fail there.)

CODEGEN: tools/dataclass_reflection_sites.py  (build: main: 'DEFAULT_FILES' has storage here — it is one of the module-global slots in this image's `__DATA`, because a function writes it through `global DEFAULT_FILES` — but that storage has no initializer: one of its elements is computed by a call, and a container is laid out here as a run of words — so an element that is not a word has nothing to put in the blob. An int element, a string element and a NESTED container of those three all do (the string as a pointer the initializer fills in, the nested container as a blob of its own with one more fixup for the word that points at it); a call's result is not known before the program runs, and no arrangement of `__DATA` changes that. A slot is eight bytes of static storage, so its value has to be known before the program runs; one that is not would read as the zero an unwritten slot gives, which is a plausible wrong number rather than a refusal)
CODEGEN: tools/detrace_diff.py  (build: sys.exit(): `sys` is a linked module but it exports no `exit`, so the call has no symbol to bind. What it does export: api_version, byteorder, flush_output, get_coroutine_origin_tracking_depth, get_int_max_str_digits, getdefaultencoding, getfilesystemencodeerrors, getfilesystemencoding, …. A name `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule excludes (a leading `_`, a generic template, an overload, or a C library symbol such as `exit` or `write`) is not advertised, so a module that defines one cannot be called by that name. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md 1.1; a module that needs to publish mutable state rather than functions is a different gap, written down in bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md)

CODEGEN: tools/gatewatch.py  (build: __module_body__: sys.argv reads 'argv' out of the imported module `sys`, and a module is not a value this path can place: there is no register, frame slot or `__DATA` word for it because it is not one — it is the library on this link line, and what a dylib publishes is its FUNCTIONS (as symbols, so `sys.fn(...)` lowers) and its module-level names the build FOLDED TO A LITERAL (as values, so `sys.K = 1` lowers — there is exactly one value of a folded module-level name in a whole program, so the importer materializes the same one). What it cannot publish is a VARIABLE, and `argv` is one: a list, an object or a stream has no representation as a word on the other side of the boundary, because every value a formal program can name lives in a function's own stack scratch, which is reclaimed when the function returns. What `sys` publishes: api_version, byteorder, flush_output, get_coroutine_origin_tracking_depth, get_int_max_str_digits, getdefaultencoding, getfilesystemencodeerrors, getfilesystemencoding, …. `bugs/FORMAL_module_state_no_storage.md` records the design and what would have to be true to close it, and §(2) is this shape)

Its effects would be silently missing: the image would build, exit 0, and not be the program you wrote, which is the one failure a value this compiler cannot detect on its own. An arm whose body is `pass`, `raise`, `continue` or `break` still builds — nothing is lost by leaving it out. Otherwise: move the work into the `try` body or after the statement (a `finally` if it is cleanup for the success path), or end the arm with `raise` if the program is meant to fail there.)

CODEGEN: tools/wave1_move_shared.py  (build: __module_body__: `Path` is called, and it is imported from `pathlib`, so the call has to bind a symbol `pathlib` exports. That module does not export it, and the reason is `doc/ABI.md`'s export rule rather than anything about this call: a name with a leading `_` is private, a generic template is not one symbol but one per instantiation (`_get_kgen_string[asm]()` is the measured case — both at once), an overload has no single symbol, and a C library name like `exit` or `write` is provided by libSystem. The exclusion is measured and settled in bugs/FORMAL_known_limits.md §1.1, and the generic case in §1.2. A generic template's instantiations ARE compiled into that module's library when an importer asks for them (`formal/monomorph.py`), so this call is one that asked for none — it names no type argument, or names one that is a value rather than a type, or spells the template as `module.Path`; spell it as `Path[<a type>](…)` and the library will carry the instantiation. Write the operation in this module, or call a public function that does it — which is the same program with a definition this image can bind)
